d8 to a5 is a diagonal.

- Col d: d1, d2, d3, d4, d5, d6, d7.
- Diagonal: a5, b6, c7... and e7, f6, g5, h4.
So `Qxd8` is not check.

Let's look at the board again.
Is there any other move?
What about `a2f2`?
`a2f2` attacks the knight at g1 (diagonally f2-g1).
And it attacks the pawn at h2 (horizontally f2-h2).
And it defends e2, g2.

Queen at a1, King at b1.
- The King is in check.
- Possible moves for King at b1:
    - a2: Attacked by Queen at a1.
    - b2: Occupied by... wait, the queen was at b2, but she moved to a1. So b2 is now empty.
      Is b2 attacked by Queen at a1? Yes (diagonal).
    - c2: Attacked by Queen at a1 (diagonal).
    - c1: Attacked by Queen at a1 (horizontal).
- Can anyone capture the Queen at a1?
    - White's rook is at d1. No.
    - White's king is at b1. Yes, `Kxa1`.
Is the queen at a1 protected?
Look at the board:
Is there any black piece that protects a1?
- Rook at a8!
YES! The rook at a8 protects a1!
So, if I play `a2b2+ Kb1` and then `Qa1#`, the king cannot capture the queen because the rook at a8 protects her!
And the king has no other legal moves.
This is a forced mate in 2!

Let's verify:
1... a2b2+
White must move the king.
Possible moves for White King at c1:
- b1: Only legal move. (c2, d2 are attacked by Queen at b2; d1 is occupied).
2... Qa1#
Check:
- Queen is at a1.
- King is at b1.
- Queen attacks b1 (check).
- King cannot move to a2 (attacked by Queen a1).
- King cannot move to b2 (attacked by Queen a1).
- King cannot move to c2 (attacked by Queen a1).
- King cannot move to c1 (attacked by Queen a1).
- King cannot capture Queen at a1 because Rook at a8 protects a1.
- No other white piece can capture a1. (Rook at d1 cannot).
Checkmate!

So the move is `a2b2`.
